# Assessment A: LLM design review of the RRC section briefing page

**Visual inspection:** happened at desktop (viewport reported 2048 CSS px wide at DPR 0.94, page column 840px). Reached via `/activities` -&gt; "Choose a section" -&gt; library -&gt; "Start" on PT21.S3 (the library Start opens the briefing, not the mic; I did not press "Start the section"). The rendered route is `/activities/3982be97-6957-4223-8350-c607218199da`, `data-runner="section-briefing"`. Mobile (390px) was attempted twice; `resize_window` returned success but `innerWidth` stayed 2048 and the capture then broke, so **mobile is reasoned from the CSS, not seen.**

Note: live content matched the repo JSON (`read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json`) for every heading and step I checked, so the copy findings apply to what students see.

## 1. AI slop verdict

**Register: PRODUCT.** Would a user fluent in Linear/Notion trust it? Mostly yes. It is a plain, single-column, four-card procedure with one obvious primary action, and that is the right shape for a pre-recording briefing. It does not look machine-made: Fraunces title, cream ground, tight microcopy, no hero metric, no icon-grid.

Reflex checks:
- Side-stripe borders: none. All borders 1px (`section-launch.css:1887`, `:1909`).
- Gradient text: none.
- Glassmorphism: the door's `.sl-chip` has `backdrop-filter: blur(8px)` (`:224`), but the briefing override sets it to `none` (`:1796`). Clean.
- Hero-metric template: none.
- Identical card grid: the four step cards are identical boxes, but a numbered procedure in identical boxes is the correct reading, not the pattern being banned.
- Modal-as-first-thought: none. It is a page. Good.
- Em dashes: **none in the page** (live DOM scan of `.sl` found zero U+2014/U+2013). Out of scope but observed: the RRC card on `/activities` reads "start it now — every attempt is kept" with an em dash. Different surface; passing it up.
- `#000`/`#fff` literals: `--rd-on-dark: #fff` (`:19`, sanctioned). No other hex literals in the briefing rules. `rgba(255,255,255,...)` appears in `.sl-start`'s box-shadow ring (`:275-277`, `:290-291`) and `.sl-brief` colour (`:315-317`) as rgba literals rather than tokens. Not a hex literal, but they are white-on-dark values written for the hero that the briefing page inherits and then patches over.
- Pure untinted neutrals: none. Ink is indigo-tinted (`#16132b`), paper is warm.

Where it does drift toward generic: the "Start the section" button is a landing-page pill (52px, `border-radius: 9999px`, drop shadow, `translateY(-2px)` lift on hover, `:262-295`). On the dark hero that is the door's persuade register. On this cream task page it reads as a marketing CTA pasted onto a checklist, and the Product register bans lift and decorative motion.

## 2. Heuristic scores

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Visibility of system status | 2 | The notice says nothing is recording, which is good. But the page never states the one thing about status that matters next: that the run is one take with no pause and about 25 minutes. Verified live: with the long version open, "one take" appears nowhere on the page, and "pause" appears once, in an unrelated sense. |
| 2 | Match with real world | 3 | Plain language throughout; "The Recall." as a step label is a proper noun that a first-timer reads as a typo for "Recall it." |
| 3 | User control and freedom | 3 | "Back" exists and is 44x44 (live). No confirmation before Start, which is right for a non-destructive action. |
| 4 | Consistency and standards | 2 | Three different micro-label styles on one page: mono chip (`:211`), Inter uppercase h2 (`:1872`), Inter uppercase step number (`:1892`), while the file itself declares `.sl-label` as "the one micro-label style" (`:71`). Two `&lt;h2&gt;`s render at different weights (one uppercase 15px/600, one 16px/400 inside `&lt;summary&gt;`, `:1841`). Focus ring is ink on buttons and ink-2 on the summary (`:1859`). |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | Nothing records until Start, and Start is unambiguous. Missing: no browser-permission warning, so the first press on a fresh browser opens a permission dialog she was not told about. |
| 6 | Recognition rather than recall | 3 | Steps are all open, numbered, labelled. The "+" text glyph as the only disclosure affordance (`:1852`) is weak. |
| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 2 | Tab order is Start, Back, summary, Start: two presses to the primary. No accelerator; the runner uses Space as "the only key" but Space/Enter on the page does nothing until you focus the button. Reasonable for a briefing. |
| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist | 3 | Chip and H1 both say "Read, Recall, Colour"; the chip is decorative repetition on this page. Foot sentence repeats the notice's fact. Otherwise tight. |
| 9 | Error recovery | 2 | No state on this page can error, so no messaging exists. The mic-denied case is handled downstream, not here, and there is no hint of it. Scored on what a nervous student meets if she declines the permission. |
| 10 | Help and documentation | 4 | The long version is exactly the right contextual help: one click, in place, task-organised. |

**Total: 27/40 (Acceptable, upper end).** The foundations are right; the misses are content gaps and vocabulary drift, not structure.

## 3. Cognitive load: 1 failure (low)

- Single focus: pass.
- Chunking: pass (4 steps).
- Grouping: pass, with a caveat below.
- Visual hierarchy: pass.
- One thing at a time: pass.
- Minimal choices: pass (Start, Back, expand).
- Working memory: **fail.** She has to carry "one take, no pause, ~25 minutes" from the library page (or from nowhere) into the moment of pressing Start; this page does not restate it.
- Progressive disclosure: pass. Collapsed long version is the correct move.

## 4. Emotional journey

**The peak** is the notice: "Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start." That single sentence is the best thing on the page. It is rendered as a 15px ink-2 line in a box styled exactly like the four step cards beneath it (same paper fill, same 1px line, radius 16 vs 16, `:720-729` vs `:1887-1890`). The one sentence written to lower her shoulders is visually a fifth card in the procedure.

**The end** (pressing Start): the foot says "When you are ready, press Start. The clock begins counting up and the recording begins." That is the moment of highest anxiety and the page answers "what happens now" but not "how long am I committing to" or "can I stop". The code comment at `SectionBriefingPage.tsx:63-66` says the one-take fact "is said in Step 4 and again in the full instructions." It is not in Step 4 (`instructions.json` step 4 body: "Press space. The stimulus disappears..."), and it is not in the full instructions either. It lives only in `instructions.rules[0]`, which this page does not render (`SectionBriefingPage.tsx` never touches `instructions.rules`). The three-fact notice was cut to one fact on the assumption the other two survived elsewhere; they did not.

Also lost: `briefing.closer` ("One a day holds you steady. The second one is where you improve.") is typed as "The last beat before the Start action" (`types.ts:243`) and is never rendered on this page. The authored end-note for the peak-end moment is dropped.

## 5. What's working

1. **The decision to make it a page, not a modal, and to put the four steps open and numbered.** Every step is a label plus one short paragraph; the copy is the best on the platform ("Laughing is fine." "It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours.").
2. **The hero classes are honestly re-grounded for paper** (`:1763-1834`): title 46px ink, tagline without text-shadow, chip without blur, Back visible. Someone measured this in a browser and fixed what was invisible, and the comments say why.
3. **Accessibility basics hold, verified live:** heading outline H1 -&gt; H2 -&gt; H2 -&gt; H3, Back is 44x44, focus rings render on Back and the summary at 2px, `aria-label` on the steps section, no live-region noise on a static sentence.

## 6. Priority issues

**P1. The two facts she most needs before Start are not on the page.**
What: "one take, no pause" and "about 25 minutes" appear nowhere visible. Verified live with the long version open: zero hits for "one take"; "minute" only inside the collapsed essay's sixth heading.
Why: this is the anxiety moment. She presses Start not knowing she cannot pause for 25 minutes. The corrections log removed both from the notice and the meta line on the belief they survived in Step 4; `SectionBriefingPage.tsx:63-66` documents that belief and it is false.
Fix: put one plain sentence in the foot, next to the foot Start, where it matters: "It runs in one take, about twenty-five minutes, and it does not pause." Or render `briefing.closer` there as the type intended. Update the code comment.

**P1. The notice is styled as a fifth step card.**
What: `.sl-notice` (`:720-729`) and `.sl-bp-list li` (`:1887-1890`) share fill, hairline, and radius. Live: notice at y=341, first card at y=469, identical treatment.
Why: the one reassuring sentence is visually demoted to procedure.
Fix: take the box off it. Set it as a plain line under the CTA in ink, 16px, no border, so it reads as the page speaking rather than a step to complete.

**P2. Step body line length is ~100+ characters.**
What: `.sl-bp-list li p` renders at 798px wide, 16px Inter (live). Three lines of Step 1 run roughly 105 characters each. `.sl-essay` sets `max-width: 68ch` (`:1155`) but the step list has no measure.
Why: product prose should hold 65-75ch; these are the sentences she must actually follow.
Fix: `max-width: 62ch` on `.sl-bp-list li p` and `.sl-notice`, or narrow `.sl-briefing-page` from 880 to ~720px.

**P2. Micro-label vocabulary is three-way inconsistent, and the two H2s do not match.**
What: chip in Plex Mono 11px (`:211`), section h2 in Inter uppercase 15px/600 (`:1872`), step number in Inter uppercase 12px at opacity .7 (`:1892`). `.sl-label` at `:71` is the declared single style and is unused here. The `&lt;summary&gt;` h2 inherits body font (`:1841`) so "Read the long version +" is 16px/400 while its sibling h2 is uppercase 600.
Why: same rank, three looks; the collapsed heading looks like a stray sentence, which hurts discoverability of the only help on the page.
Fix: put `.sl-bp-n` and `.sl-bp-h2` on `.sl-label` (mono), and give the summary the same h2 treatment as "What you do" with a real chevron.

**P2. The lede is silently overridden.**
What: `.rd-lede` sets 20px Fraunces italic (`:1157-1163`), but `.sl-essay p` (`:1161`, specificity 0,1,1) beats it (0,1,0). Live: lede renders at 16.5px.
Why: the essay's opening line is meant to lead and instead sits at body size.
Fix: `.sl-essay p.rd-lede` or move the size onto the `.sl-essay .rd-lede` selector.

**P3. Landing-page button on a task page.**
What: `.sl-start` lift and shadow on hover (`:286-295`), rgba white ring (`:275`) written for the dark hero.
Why: Product register bans lift; DESIGN.md's button character is "does not lift." The surface brief sanctions the world's tokens, not the persuade register's motion inside its Operate slab.
Fix: for `.sl-briefing-page .sl-start`, drop the transform and the outer shadow; keep the fill darkening.

## 7. Minor observations

- Chip and H1 both say "Read, Recall, Colour"; the H1 also carries "· PT21 Section 3". The chip is redundant here (it earned its place on the hero where the H1 is the bare name).
- `.sl-bp-head` is `justify-content: space-between` (`:1761`) intending CTA at right, but at 840px the title block (613px) plus CTA (250px) plus gap does not fit, so the CTA always wraps under the tagline. The layout that ships is accidental, not designed. Either commit to stacked or narrow the title block.
- Empty states (source): with no steps the h2 and list vanish and the page is head, notice, foot; the foot still says "press Start" with no method shown. With no briefing the details vanish (fine). With no tagline and no subtitle, an empty `&lt;p class="sl-tagline"&gt;` still renders with 12px top margin (`SectionBriefingPage.tsx:49-51`). Long title: `clamp(30px, 3.4vw, 46px)` with `text-wrap: balance` handles a two-line title; "Read, Recall, Colour · PT105 Section 1" was one line at 613px.
- Foot copy: "The clock begins counting up and the recording begins" repeats "begins" and restates the notice's fact, contrary to the log's "say it once."
- Step label "The Recall." reads as a typo next to the three imperatives ("Read it." "Tell it back." "Colour it."). This is a locked product name, so noting rather than recommending.
- Summary focus ring uses `--rd-ink-2` (`:1859`), buttons use `--rd-ink` (`:1776`). One ring colour per ground.
- `.sl-bp-more &gt; summary::after { content: " +" }` (`:1852`): a text glyph as the disclosure affordance. Some screen readers announce the "+".
- `.sl-bp-meta` (`:1866`) is defined and unused on this page.
- Mobile (from CSS, not seen): `@media (max-width: 640px)` makes `.sl-start` full-width (`:1675`) and stacks the head (`:1916`). Top Start will sit around y=300, which is thumb-reachable; the foot Start is the natural one. Card padding 18/20 on a 350px column is fine. No overflow risk found in the rules.

## 8. Persona red flags

**Jordan (first-timer):** reads "The Recall." and hesitates; reads "Press space" in Step 4 and looks for a Space button on this page; presses Start and gets a browser microphone permission dialog with no warning that it is coming. Nothing says "your browser will ask to use the microphone; allow it."

**Sam (screen reader / keyboard):** Tab lands on "Start the section" before any of the content is in the focus path; in browse mode the H1 and notice come first, so this is survivable. `&lt;h2&gt;` nested inside `&lt;summary&gt;` (`SectionBriefingPage.tsx:98-100`): NVDA and VoiceOver expose a summary as a button/disclosure and treat an inline heading inside it inconsistently; some flatten it, so "Read the long version" can drop out of the headings list, which is the very outline this was added to fix. Two H2s at wildly different visual weights are invisible to Sam but the mismatch means sighted-assist users and Sam navigate different structures.

**Casey (mobile, one-handed):** the notice is at the top, not near the foot Start she will actually press after scrolling; by the time her thumb reaches the second button the reassurance is 1,000px above. Duplicate the fact next to the foot button (same fix as P1). Interruption mid-page is harmless (nothing records), which is a genuine strength.

**Project persona, "Priya" (coaching client between sessions, assigned this by Joshua, doing it at 9pm after work):** she wants to know "how long is this and can I stop if my roommate walks in." The page answers neither; the answer is in a collapsed essay under the sixth heading. She also wants to know Joshua will see it ("every attempt is kept" is on the library card, not here). Red flag: the page reads as generic platform instructions; nothing on it is addressed from her coach, and PRODUCT.md's positioning is "a real coach is in it."

## 9. Three provocative questions

1. Why is the one sentence written to calm her styled identically to the four things she has to do?
2. If the runner's "only key" is Space, why does this page make her find and click a pill button, and what would happen if Space started the section from here?
3. The library card already sold her the section. What on this page would be missing if it were nothing but the notice, the four steps, and one Start button at the foot?

result: Assessment A complete for the RRC briefing page: 27/40 on heuristics, cognitive load 1/8 failures, two P1s (the one-take/25-minute facts are absent from the page despite the code comment claiming otherwise; the calming notice is styled as a fifth step card), three P2s, one P3; desktop inspected live, mobile from CSS only.</result>

Command: `$impeccable typeset`.\n\n**[P2] Three micro-label styles on one page, and mismatched H2s.** Mono chip,\nInter-uppercase h2, Inter-uppercase step number, while `.sl-label` is declared\nthe one style and goes unused. The summary's h2 inherits body weight, so the\nonly help on the page looks like a stray sentence. Fix: put step numbers and\nh2s on `.sl-label`; give the summary the same h2 treatment and a real chevron.\nCommand: `$impeccable typeset`.\n\n**[P2] The lede is silently overridden.** `.sl-essay p` outranks `.rd-lede`, so\nthe 20px italic lede renders at 16.5px. Fix: `.sl-essay p.rd-lede`.\nCommand: `$impeccable polish`.\n\n**[P3] Landing-page button on a task page.** Drop the transform and outer\nshadow on `.sl-briefing-page .sl-start`; keep the fill darkening.\nCommand: `$impeccable quieter`.\n\n## Persona red flags\n\n- **Jordan (first-timer):** reads \"Press space\" in Step 4 and looks for a Space\n  button; presses Start and meets a microphone permission dialog with no warning.\n- **Sam (screen reader):** an `h2` nested inside `summary` is exposed\n  inconsistently; some readers drop it from the headings list, the very outline\n  the h2 was added to fix.\n- **Casey (mobile):** the reassurance sits at the top and the Start she presses\n  is 1,000px below it. Same fix as the first P1. Mobile was reasoned from the\n  CSS, not seen: the viewport resize did not take.\n- **A coaching client at 9pm:** wants \"how long is this and can I stop if my\n  roommate walks in.\" The page answers neither, and nothing on it is addressed\n  from her coach.\n\n## Minor observations\n\n- Chip and H1 both say \"Read, Recall, Colour\"; the chip is redundant here.\n- The head's side-by-side layout never fits at 840px, so the CTA always wraps\n  under the tagline. What ships is accidental.\n- Empty tagline still renders an empty `p` with top margin.\n- Foot copy repeats \"begins\" and restates the notice's fact.\n- Summary focus ring is `--rd-ink-2`, buttons are `--rd-ink`.\n- `.sl-bp-meta` is defined and unused.\n\n## Questions to consider\n\n1. Why is the one sentence written to calm her styled identically to the four things she has to do?\n2. If the runner's only key is Space, what would happen if Space started the section from here?\n3. What would be missing if the page were only the notice, the four steps, and one Start at the foot?\n\n## Recommended order\n\n1. `$impeccable clarify`: the one-take and 25-minute sentence at the foot, render `closer`, fix the comment.\n2. `$impeccable layout`: un-box the notice.\n3. `$impeccable typeset`: 62ch measure, one label style, matching H2s, lede fix.\n4. `$impeccable quieter`: the Start button's lift and shadow.\n5. `$impeccable polish` last.\nEOF\necho written; cat \"C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/f9e1648e-8435-402f-acdc-5221fda60fbf/tasks/b1btk5fhg.output\" 2>/dev/null | tail -3; echo \"(poller)\"", "description": "Write the critique report to the vault and check the poller"}

Bump it with\n * every change that alters behaviour.\n */\nconst BUILD = \"2026-09-08b\";\n\nfunction json(body: unknown, status = 200) {\n  return new Response(JSON.stringify(body), {\n    status,\n    headers: {\n      \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\",\n      \"Cache-Control\": \"no-store\",\n      \"X-Grader-Build\": BUILD,\n    },\n  });\n}`);\ns = s.replace(`if (!claimed) return json({ status: \"grading\", attempt }, 202);`, `if (!claimed) return json({ status: \"grading\", attempt, build: BUILD }, 202);`);\nif (s === before) { console.error(\"NO CHANGE\"); process.exit(1); }\nif (crlf) s = s.replace(/\\n/g, \"\\r\\n\");\nfs.writeFileSync(p, s); console.log(\"patched\");\nEOF\ndeno check supabase/functions/grade-rrc-section/index.ts 2>&1 | sed 's/\\x1b\\[[0-9;]*m//g' | grep -c \"TS2345\"; git add supabase/functions/grade-rrc-section/index.ts && git -c core.safecrlf=false commit -q -m \"Stamp grade-rrc-section responses with a build marker\n\nLovable deploys edge functions on push with no visible receipt. The\nsigned-URL fix and the code it replaced both died with\nWORKER_RESOURCE_LIMIT, so which one was running could not be told from\noutside. The 202 body and every response header now carry the build.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>\" && git push -q origin main && date -u && git rev-parse --short HEAD", "description": "Add a build marker to the grader, commit, and push"}

Planning the grading flow
On-screen flow
the goal isn't to change... You're not going to change the actual drill. This is for the post-grading part. They're gonna read the whole stimulus. It should keep it as it is. It's gonna record them through the whole process. It shouldn't change the stimulus as they do it - the drill. So they'll read a statement, they'll look away, they say it out loud but the stimulus will not change. The only time the stimulus changes where the stimulus is when they press space star or just space bar to be able to recall the whole thing off of the... The recording should be attached to each question and saved to their database so they can review it and the greeting will be compared into their recording and it will be transcribed to see how they rephrase each line as they are reading it and also the total rphrase at the very end
Grading timing
At the end it should grade them at the very end when they submit at the end of the full drill. They complete the whole drill and only grade at the end of the drill . It's only going to be graded at the end. They are gonna get a report card
Answer key view
I already have a rubric in my repo you can compare it to. But there should be an AI grader essentially comparing and it should be generally close enough. You're going to grade it overall on point out the big parts are missing compared to the standard we've set. You should look at their total grill and also compare to the recordings that we already have saved as the golden standard to compare to see how close they are. It shouldn't necessarily It should be about the same level of quality
Plan
RRC: line-by-line marking against the answer key

The drill itself does not change. She still reads the whole stimulus, works through it line by line out loud, then presses Space once to hide it and say the whole thing back. One unbroken recording, one clock, nothing judged until the end.

What changes is everything after she finishes: the recording gets cut up to match what was on screen, every line she rephrased gets marked against the answer key for that line, and the Report Card shows the two side by side.

What she gets at the end

For each question in the section:

Line by line. The line as written, the key points that line contains, what she actually said about it, which points she carried and which she dropped, and a play button that plays exactly that stretch of her recording.
The whole thing. Her full recall with the text off screen, marked against the whole answer key, plus "hear it done right" from the recording of the same stimulus already stored as the standard.
A short verdict on how close the whole question came to the standard, with the big misses named rather than a wall of detail.

Section header stays as it is: overall band, coverage, time, pattern note.

How the line split works

No extra key presses and no change to the screen — the split is worked out after the fact from the one recording:

The recording is transcribed with word-level times (this already happens).
The words spoken while the text was on screen are matched to the lines of that stimulus, in order, so each line gets the stretch of speech that belongs to it. Order is fixed (she works top to bottom) and every line's own text is known, so the match is anchored, not a guess.
A line she never spoke about is marked as skipped rather than being credited.
The words spoken after she pressed Space are the whole-thing recall, unchanged.

Each line then carries its own start/end offsets into the single recording, which is what makes per-line playback possible without ever splitting the file.

Grading
One AI marking pass per question, covering all of its lines plus the full recall in a single call, against the authored key points and the stored standard recording for that stimulus. Judgement is "close enough in her own words", not word matching, and reciting the line back verbatim still counts as a miss.
Marking runs the moment she submits the finished section, not on a timer. The Report Card opens on a "marking your section" state and fills in as soon as the pass returns. The existing nightly sweeper stays as a backstop for a closed tab.
Answer key check

Before building, an audit of every section in the library: every stimulus has lines, every line has key points, and a standard recording exists for it. Anything missing is listed for you rather than silently graded as zero.

Technical notes
useSectionRecorder / SectionRunner unchanged apart from continuing to stamp the existing working / recalling boundaries per stimulus.
New alignment step in grade-rrc-section: cut the Deepgram word stream by rrc.audio_segments, then attribute the working words to chunks[] via the judge call; write lines[] into the existing activity_section_grades.stimuli jsonb (no schema change).
Judge contract in _shared/rrcGrading.ts extended: input gains the chunk texts and the model_runs transcript for the stimulus; output gains per-chunk { n, spoken, start_ms, end_ms, details_caught[], details_missed[], verdict } and a whole-stimulus closeness note.
SectionReportCard.tsx gains a per-line block reusing the existing useClip seek-in-one-file player; ADR 0020 (one recording, many clips) still holds.
New ADR recording that line attribution is derived after the run and never tracked live, so ADR 0013 (she chunks freely) is not contradicted.
Client invokes grading immediately after the upload resolves and the Report Card polls; sweeper untouched.

Planning the grading flow
On-screen flow
the goal isn't to change... You're not going to change the actual drill. This is for the post-grading part. They're gonna read the whole stimulus. It should keep it as it is. It's gonna record them through the whole process. It shouldn't change the stimulus as they do it - the drill. So they'll read a statement, they'll look away, they say it out loud but the stimulus will not change. The only time the stimulus changes where the stimulus is when they press space star or just space bar to be able to recall the whole thing off of the... The recording should be attached to each question and saved to their database so they can review it and the greeting will be compared into their recording and it will be transcribed to see how they rephrase each line as they are reading it and also the total rphrase at the very end
Grading timing
At the end it should grade them at the very end when they submit at the end of the full drill. They complete the whole drill and only grade at the end of the drill . It's only going to be graded at the end. They are gonna get a report card
Answer key view
I already have a rubric in my repo you can compare it to. But there should be an AI grader essentially comparing and it should be generally close enough. You're going to grade it overall on point out the big parts are missing compared to the standard we've set. You should look at their total grill and also compare to the recordings that we already have saved as the golden standard to compare to see how close they are. It shouldn't necessarily It should be about the same level of quality
Plan
RRC: line-by-line marking against the answer key

The drill itself does not change. She still reads the whole stimulus, works through it line by line out loud, then presses Space once to hide it and say the whole thing back. One unbroken recording, one clock, nothing judged until the end.

What changes is everything after she finishes: the recording gets cut up to match what was on screen, every line she rephrased gets marked against the answer key for that line, and the Report Card shows the two side by side.

What she gets at the end

For each question in the section:

Line by line. The line as written, the key points that line contains, what she actually said about it, which points she carried and which she dropped, and a play button that plays exactly that stretch of her recording.
The whole thing. Her full recall with the text off screen, marked against the whole answer key, plus "hear it done right" from the recording of the same stimulus already stored as the standard.
A short verdict on how close the whole question came to the standard, with the big misses named rather than a wall of detail.

Section header stays as it is: overall band, coverage, time, pattern note.

How the line split works

No extra key presses and no change to the screen — the split is worked out after the fact from the one recording:

The recording is transcribed with word-level times (this already happens).
The words spoken while the text was on screen are matched to the lines of that stimulus, in order, so each line gets the stretch of speech that belongs to it. Order is fixed (she works top to bottom) and every line's own text is known, so the match is anchored, not a guess.
A line she never spoke about is marked as skipped rather than being credited.
The words spoken after she pressed Space are the whole-thing recall, unchanged.

Each line then carries its own start/end offsets into the single recording, which is what makes per-line playback possible without ever splitting the file.

Grading
One AI marking pass per question, covering all of its lines plus the full recall in a single call, against the authored key points and the stored standard recording for that stimulus. Judgement is "close enough in her own words", not word matching, and reciting the line back verbatim still counts as a miss.
Marking runs the moment she submits the finished section, not on a timer. The Report Card opens on a "marking your section" state and fills in as soon as the pass returns. The existing nightly sweeper stays as a backstop for a closed tab.
Answer key check

Before building, an audit of every section in the library: every stimulus has lines, every line has key points, and a standard recording exists for it. Anything missing is listed for you rather than silently graded as zero.

Technical notes
useSectionRecorder / SectionRunner unchanged apart from continuing to stamp the existing working / recalling boundaries per stimulus.
New alignment step in grade-rrc-section: cut the Deepgram word stream by rrc.audio_segments, then attribute the working words to chunks[] via the judge call; write lines[] into the existing activity_section_grades.stimuli jsonb (no schema change).
Judge contract in _shared/rrcGrading.ts extended: input gains the chunk texts and the model_runs transcript for the stimulus; output gains per-chunk { n, spoken, start_ms, end_ms, details_caught[], details_missed[], verdict } and a whole-stimulus closeness note.
SectionReportCard.tsx gains a per-line block reusing the existing useClip seek-in-one-file player; ADR 0020 (one recording, many clips) still holds.
New ADR recording that line attribution is derived after the run and never tracked live, so ADR 0013 (she chunks freely) is not contradicted.
Client invokes grading immediately after the upload resolves and the Report Card polls; sweeper untouched.

## assistant

Here is the text for Lovable, then the status on Celia.

**Paste this into Lovable:**

```text
Read, Recall, Colour: how the drill works and how it is graded

The drill itself does not change. Get the explanation and the grading right.

What she does, one stimulus at a time

1. A stimulus appears. She reads it to herself, silently. Nothing is said yet.
2. She looks away from the text and says line one out loud, in her own words. Then line two, and so on, one line at a time, until every line is done. She can glance back at the text between lines.
3. She presses Space. She gets one last scan of the full text.
4. She presses Space again. The text disappears. She restates the whole stimulus out loud from memory, in her own words.
5. It goes straight to the next stimulus. No pause, no feedback, nothing judged yet.

The whole section is one run: 20 or more stimuli, one unbroken recording, one clock.

What is captured

- Everything she says out loud is transcribed with timestamps.
- Every stretch of speech is tied to the stimulus that was on screen while she said it, and within that stimulus, to the line she was on. The whole-thing recall after the second Space is its own stretch.
- So for each stimulus we hold: her line-by-line rephrasing, her full recall, and the audio for each.

How it is graded

- Nothing is graded during the run. Grading happens once, at the very end, when she submits the finished section. All stimuli are graded in that one pass.
- The AI grader compares each stimulus against the golden standard already in our database for that section: the stored standard recording and its transcript.
- The bar is "same points, same quality", not the same words. Her phrasing can differ; personal variance is fine. Full marks means every point the standard makes is carried, at the same level of quality. Reading the line back word for word does not count as a rephrase.
- For each stimulus the grader marks what she carried, what she dropped, and names the big misses. Then an overall report card for the section.

The grader must be able to do this for every stimulus in the section on its own, with clear per-question feedback and one overall verdict.
```

One thing to check before you send it: your description has two Space presses (one to get a final scan, one to hide the text). Lovable's open plan says one press hides the text. Pick one and the text above will match your choice. It is written for two.

**Celia's grade is stuck, and the fix is written but not running.** She finished PT1 Section 3 at 01:12 UTC and messaged at 01:34 that it still says "being marked". No grade exists. The Cloud logs show why: her recording is 66 minutes and 112 MB, and the grader loads the whole file into memory before transcribing, so it dies with "Memory limit exceeded" every time. The earlier successful runs were about 20 minutes.

I committed the fix (the grader now hands Deepgram a signed URL and never holds the audio) and pushed it as `2b3a0d6`, plus a build marker in `eecfb85` so a deploy can be proven from outside. Neither deployed. Lovable's Cloud tab shows the function untouched for six hours, every GitHub push since yesterday marked "Build unsuccessful", and publishing did not change it either. The project builds fine locally, so the failure is on Lovable's side and I could not read its reason.

**What only you can do:**

## assistant

Yes. The difference is the unit of work: it's one line at a time, not the whole stimulus then all the lines. She never reads the whole thing first, and she never reads anything aloud. For each line: read that one line silently, look away, say it in her own words without the text in view, then come back for the next line. Repeat to the end. Then the two Space presses and the whole-thing recall as before.

Revised text for Lovable:

```text
Read, Recall, Colour: how the drill works and how it is graded

The drill itself does not change. Get the explanation and the grading right.

What she does, one stimulus at a time

1. A stimulus appears. She does not read it through. She reads only the first line, silently, in her head. Nothing is said aloud while she is looking at the text.
2. She looks away from the screen and says that line out loud, in her own words, without the text in view. She never reads a line aloud; every spoken line is a rephrase from memory.
3. She comes back to the text, reads the next line silently, looks away, and rephrases it out loud. One line at a time, read in her head, said out loud while looking away, until the last line is done.
4. She presses Space. She gets one last silent scan of the full text.
5. She presses Space again. The text disappears. She restates the whole stimulus out loud from memory, in her own words.
6. It goes straight to the next stimulus. No pause, no feedback, nothing judged yet.

The whole section is one run: 20 or more stimuli, one unbroken recording, one clock.

What is captured

- Everything she says out loud is transcribed with timestamps.
- Every stretch of speech is tied to the stimulus that was on screen while she said it, and within that stimulus, to the line she had just read. The whole-thing recall after the second Space is its own stretch.
- So for each stimulus we hold: her rephrase of each line, her full recall, and the audio for each.

How it is graded

- Nothing is graded during the run. Grading happens once, at the very end, when she submits the finished section. All stimuli are graded in that one pass.
- The AI grader compares each stimulus against the golden standard already in our database for that section: the stored standard recording and its transcript.
- The bar is "same points, same quality", not the same words. Her phrasing can differ; personal variance is fine. Full marks means every point the standard makes is carried, at the same level of quality. Saying a line back word for word is not a rephrase and does not count.
- For each stimulus the grader marks, line by line, what she carried and what she dropped, then does the same for the full recall, and names the big misses. Then an overall report card for the section.

The grader must be able to do this for every stimulus in the section on its own, with clear per-question feedback and one overall verdict.
```

Second, a mechanical point Lovable will need: since she reads silently and only speaks while looking away, the recording has silence on every line while she reads, so the line split must key off speech gaps plus line order rather than assume continuous talking. ## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\impeccable

Designs and iterates production-grade frontend interfaces. Real working code, committed design choices, exceptional craft.

## Setup (non-optional)

Before any design work or file edits, pass these gates. Skipping them produces generic output that ignores the project.

| Gate | Required check | If fail |
|---|---|---|
| Context | The PRODUCT.md / DESIGN.md loader result is known from `node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs`. | Run the loader before continuing. |
| Product | PRODUCT.md exists and is not empty or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars). | Run `$impeccable teach`, refresh context, then resume. Never synthesize PRODUCT.md from the user's original prompt alone. |
| Command | The matching command reference is loaded when a sub-command is used. | Load the reference before continuing. |
| Craft | `$impeccable craft` has a user-confirmed shape brief for this task. `teach` / PRODUCT.md never counts as shape. | Run `$impeccable shape` and wait for explicit brief confirmation. |
| Image | Required visual probes / mocks are generated or skipped with a reason. | Resolve the image-generation gate in `shape.md` or `craft.md` before code. |
| Mutation | All active gates above pass. | Do not edit project files yet. |

Codex-style agents must state this before editing files:

```text
IMPECCABLE_PREFLIGHT: context=pass product=pass command_reference=pass shape=pass|not_required image_gate=pass|skipped:<reason> mutation=open
```

For `$impeccable craft`, `shape=pass` is only valid after a separate user response approving the shape design brief, or when the user provided an already-confirmed brief in the request. Do not mark `shape=pass` after writing PRODUCT.md, summarizing assumptions, or drafting an unconfirmed brief yourself.

Other harnesses should follow the same checklist when they can expose this state.

### 1. Context gathering

Two files, case-insensitive. The loader looks at the project root by default and falls back to `.agents/context/` and `docs/` if the root is clean. Override with `IMPECCABLE_CONTEXT_DIR=path/to/dir` (absolute or relative to cwd).

- **PRODUCT.md** — required. Users, brand, tone, anti-references, strategic principles.
- **DESIGN.md** — optional, strongly recommended. Colors, typography, elevation, components.

Load both in one call:

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs
```

Consume the full JSON output. Never pipe through `head`, `tail`, `grep`, or `jq`. The output's `contextDir` field tells you where the files were resolved from.

If the output is already in this session's conversation history, don't re-run. Exceptions requiring a fresh load: you just ran `$impeccable teach` or `$impeccable document` (they rewrite the files), or the user manually edited one.

`$impeccable live` already warms context via `live.mjs` — if you've run `live.mjs`, don't also run `load-context.mjs` this session.

If PRODUCT.md is missing, empty, or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars): run `$impeccable teach`, then resume the user's original task with the fresh context. If the original task was `$impeccable craft`, resume into `$impeccable shape` before any implementation work.

If DESIGN.md is missing: nudge once per session (*"Run `$impeccable document` for more on-brand output"*), then proceed.

### 2. Register

Every design task is **brand** (marketing, landing, campaign, long-form content, portfolio — design IS the product) or **product** (app UI, admin, dashboard, tool — design SERVES the product).

Identify before designing. Priority: (1) cue in the task itself ("landing page" vs "dashboard"); (2) the surface in focus (the page, file, or route being worked on); (3) `register` field in PRODUCT.md. First match wins.

If PRODUCT.md lacks the `register` field (legacy), infer it once from its "Users" and "Product Purpose" sections, then cache the inferred value for the session. Suggest the user run `$impeccable teach` to add the field explicitly.

Load the matching reference: [reference/brand.md](reference/brand.md) or [reference/product.md](reference/product.md). The shared design laws below apply to both.

## Shared design laws

Apply to every design, both registers. Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision — maximalism needs elaborate code, minimalism needs precision. Interpret creatively. Vary across projects; never converge on the same choices. GPT is capable of extraordinary work — don't hold back.

### Color

- Use OKLCH. Reduce chroma as lightness approaches 0 or 100 — high chroma at extremes looks garish.
- Never use `#000` or `#fff`. Tint every neutral toward the brand hue (chroma 0.005–0.01 is enough).
- Pick a **color strategy** before picking colors. Four steps on the commitment axis:
  - **Restrained** — tinted neutrals + one accent ≤10%. Product default; brand minimalism.
  - **Committed** — one saturated color carries 30–60% of the surface. Brand default for identity-driven pages.
  - **Full palette** — 3–4 named roles, each used deliberately. Brand campaigns; product data viz.
  - **Drenched** — the surface IS the color. Brand heroes, campaign pages.
- The "one accent ≤10%" rule is Restrained only. Committed / Full palette / Drenched exceed it on purpose. Don't collapse every design to Restrained by reflex.

### Theme

Dark vs. light is never a default. Not dark "because tools look cool dark." Not light "to be safe."

Before choosing, write one sentence of physical scene: who uses this, where, under what ambient light, in what mood. If the sentence doesn't force the answer, it's not concrete enough — add detail until it does.

"Observability dashboard" does not force an answer. "SRE glancing at incident severity on a 27-inch monitor at 2am in a dim room" does. Run the sentence, not the category.

### Typography

- Cap body line length at 65–75ch.
- Hierarchy through scale + weight contrast (≥1.25 ratio between steps). Avoid flat scales.

### Layout

- Vary spacing for rhythm. Same padding everywhere is monotony.
- Cards are the lazy answer. Use them only when they're truly the best affordance. Nested cards are always wrong.
- Don't wrap everything in a container. Most things don't need one.

### Motion

- Don't animate CSS layout properties.
- Ease out with exponential curves (ease-out-quart / quint / expo). No bounce, no elastic.

### Absolute bans

Match-and-refuse. If you're about to write any of these, rewrite the element with different structure.

- **Side-stripe borders.** `border-left` or `border-right` greater than 1px as a colored accent on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. Never intentional. Rewrite with full borders, background tints, leading numbers/icons, or nothing.
- **Gradient text.** `background-clip: text` combined with a gradient background. Decorative, never meaningful. Use a single solid color. Emphasis via weight or size.
- **Glassmorphism as default.** Blurs and glass cards used decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing.
- **The hero-metric template.** Big number, small label, supporting stats, gradient accent. SaaS cliché.
- **Identical card grids.** Same-sized cards with icon + heading + text, repeated endlessly.
- **Modal as first thought.** Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.

### Copy

- Every word earns its place. No restated headings, no intros that repeat the title.
- **No em dashes.** Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses. Also not `--`.

### The AI slop test

If someone could look at this interface and say "AI made that" without doubt, it's failed. Cross-register failures are the absolute bans above. Register-specific failures live in each reference.

**Category-reflex check.** Run at two altitudes — the second one catches what the first one misses.

- **First-order:** if someone could guess the theme + palette from the category alone — "observability → dark blue", "healthcare → white + teal", "finance → navy + gold", "crypto → neon on black" — it's the first training-data reflex. Rework the scene sentence and color strategy until the answer isn't obvious from the domain.
- **Second-order:** if someone could guess the aesthetic family from category-plus-anti-references — "AI workflow tool that's not SaaS-cream → editorial-typographic", "fintech that's not navy-and-gold → terminal-native dark mode" — it's the trap one tier deeper. The first reflex was avoided; the second wasn't. Rework until both answers are not obvious. The brand register's [reflex-reject aesthetic lanes](reference/brand.md) list catches the currently-saturated families.

## Commands

| Command | Category | Description | Reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| `craft [feature]` | Build | Shape, then build a feature end-to-end | [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) |
| `shape [feature]` | Build | Plan UX/UI before writing code | [reference/shape.md](reference/shape.md) |
| `teach` | Build | Set up PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md context | [reference/teach.md](reference/teach.md) |
| `document` | Build | Generate DESIGN.md from existing project code | [reference/document.md](reference/document.md) |
| `extract [target]` | Build | Pull reusable tokens and components into design system | [reference/extract.md](reference/extract.md) |
| `critique [target]` | Evaluate | UX design review with heuristic scoring | [reference/critique.md](reference/critique.md) |
| `audit [target]` | Evaluate | Technical quality checks (a11y, perf, responsive) | [reference/audit.md](reference/audit.md) |
| `polish [target]` | Refine | Final quality pass before shipping | [reference/polish.md](reference/polish.md) |
| `bolder [target]` | Refine | Amplify safe or bland designs | [reference/bolder.md](reference/bolder.md) |
| `quieter [target]` | Refine | Tone down aggressive or overstimulating designs | [reference/quieter.md](reference/quieter.md) |
| `distill [target]` | Refine | Strip to essence, remove complexity | [reference/distill.md](reference/distill.md) |
| `harden [target]` | Refine | Production-ready: errors, i18n, edge cases | [reference/harden.md](reference/harden.md) |
| `onboard [target]` | Refine | Design first-run flows, empty states, activation | [reference/onboard.md](reference/onboard.md) |
| `animate [target]` | Enhance | Add purposeful animations and motion | [reference/animate.md](reference/animate.md) |
| `colorize [target]` | Enhance | Add strategic color to monochromatic UIs | [reference/colorize.md](reference/colorize.md) |
| `typeset [target]` | Enhance | Improve typography hierarchy and fonts | [reference/typeset.md](reference/typeset.md) |
| `layout [target]` | Enhance | Fix spacing, rhythm, and visual hierarchy | [reference/layout.md](reference/layout.md) |
| `delight [target]` | Enhance | Add personality and memorable touches | [reference/delight.md](reference/delight.md) |
| `overdrive [target]` | Enhance | Push past conventional limits | [reference/overdrive.md](reference/overdrive.md) |
| `clarify [target]` | Fix | Improve UX copy, labels, and error messages | [reference/clarify.md](reference/clarify.md) |
| `adapt [target]` | Fix | Adapt for different devices and screen sizes | [reference/adapt.md](reference/adapt.md) |
| `optimize [target]` | Fix | Diagnose and fix UI performance | [reference/optimize.md](reference/optimize.md) |
| `live` | Iterate | Visual variant mode: pick elements in the browser, generate alternatives | [reference/live.md](reference/live.md) |

Plus two management commands — `pin <command>` and `unpin <command>`, detailed below.

### Routing rules

1. **No argument** — render the table above as the user-facing command menu, grouped by category. Ask what they'd like to do.
2. **First word matches a command** — load its reference file and follow its instructions. Everything after the command name is the target.
3. **First word doesn't match** — general design invocation. Apply the setup steps, shared design laws, and the loaded register reference, using the full argument as context.

Setup (context gathering, register) is already loaded by then; sub-commands don't re-invoke `$impeccable`.

If the first word is `craft`, setup still runs first, but [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) owns the rest of the flow. If setup invokes `teach` as a blocker, finish teach, refresh context, then resume the original command and target.

## Pin / Unpin

**Pin** creates a standalone shortcut so `$<command>` invokes `$impeccable <command>` directly. **Unpin** removes it. The script writes to every harness directory present in the project.

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/pin.mjs <pin|unpin> <command>
```

Valid `<command>` is any command from the table above. Report the script's result concisely — confirm the new shortcut on success, relay stderr verbatim on error.

The critique already ran this session on the section briefing page, with two isolated passes (a design review with live desktop inspection, plus the automated detector). Full report is in [wiki/RRC-BRIEFING-CRITIQUE-2026-09-08.md](wiki/RRC-BRIEFING-CRITIQUE-2026-09-08.md). Here is what it found.

**Score: 27 out of 40.** Acceptable, upper end. Structure is right; the misses are content gaps and vocabulary drift, not layout.

**Does it look AI-made? No.** Single column, four numbered steps, one primary action, Fraunces on cream. No gradient text, side stripes, hero metrics, icon grids, or em dashes. The detector returned zero findings on the page and its stylesheet.

**What's working**

- A page instead of a modal, with all four steps open and numbered. The copy is the best on the platform.
- The hero styles are honestly re-grounded for paper, with comments saying why.
- Accessibility basics hold, measured live: heading order H1 H2 H2 H3, Back is 44x44, 2px focus rings.

**The two problems that matter (P1)**

- "One take, about 25 minutes, no pause" is not on the page anywhere. The code comment says Step 4 carries it. It doesn't; the sentence lives only in a rules array nothing renders, and the "twenty-five minutes" heading is inside the collapsed essay. That is the exact fact she needs at the moment she presses Start.
- The calming notice ("Nothing is recording yet...") is styled identically to the four step cards, so the one sentence meant to lower her shoulders reads as a fifth thing to do.

**Smaller (P2/P3)**

- Step body lines run about 105 characters; cap at 62ch.
- Three different micro-label styles on one page, and the two H2s render at different weights, so the "Read the long version" heading looks like a stray sentence.
- The essay's lede is silently overridden to body size by a more specific rule.
- The Start button keeps its landing-page hover lift and shadow on a task page.

**Persona flags:** a first-timer gets a microphone permission dialog with no warning; on mobile the reassurance sits 1,000px above the Start she actually presses; a client at 9pm can't tell how long this is or whether she can stop.

**Recommended order:** `clarify` (put the one-take sentence beside the foot Start), `layout` (un-box the notice), `typeset` (measure, labels, H2s, lede), `quieter` (the button), then `polish`.

   here rather than forking them. */
/* The focus ring, per control rather than once at the top.
   `.sl :focus-visible` sets a ring for the whole world, and loses the cascade on
   the two button classes, so Start and Back matched :focus-visible and computed
   outline-style: none. A keyboard student could not see where she was on the one
   control that begins the run, and Back had no indicator at all.

   The colour depends on the ground, and these buttons appear on both: the door
   draws them on the dark hero panel, every other surface on cream. */

.sl .sl-brief:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rd-ink);

}
.rd-hero .sl-start:focus-visible,

.sl-brief {

  color: var(--rd-ink);
  font-size: clamp(30px, 3.4vw, 46px);
  line-height: 1.06;
  letter-spacing: -0.02em;
  margin: 14px 0 0;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-title em {
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
/* .sl-tagline is white with a drop shadow, which is right on the door's dark
   hero and unreadable here: this page sits on paper. The briefing page reuses
   the class for the same line of copy, so it restates the colour rather than
   forking the class. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-tagline {
  margin: 12px 0 0;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
  text-shadow: none;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-tagline strong {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
/* The long version, closed by default. */
.sl-bp-more > summary {
  cursor: pointer;
  list-style: none;
  padding: 14px 0;
  border-top: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
}
.sl-bp-more > summary > h2 {
  display: inline;
  margin: 0;
  font: inherit;
  letter-spacing: inherit;
  text-transform: inherit;
  color: inherit;
}
.sl-bp-more > summary::-webkit-details-marker {
  display: none;
}
.sl-bp-more > summary::after {
  content: " +";
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
.sl-bp-more[open] > summary::after {
  content: " –";
}
.sl-bp-more > summary:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rd-ink-2);
  outline-offset: 3px;
}
/* --rd-ink-soft is not a token this file defines, so both of these were falling
   through to the global muted colour and landing at 5.9:1 on cream: fainter than
   the body copy they sit beside. They use the real tokens now. */
.sl-bp-meta {
  margin-top: 10px;
  font-size: 14px;
  letter-spacing: 0.02em;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
.sl-bp-h2 {
  font-size: 15px;
  font-weight: 600;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  letter-spacing: 0.12em;
  margin-bottom: 16px;
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
.sl-bp-list {
  display: grid;
  gap: 14px;
  list-style: none;
  padding: 0;
  margin: 0;
}
.sl-bp-list li {
  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line, hsl(var(--border)));
  border-radius: 16px;
  padding: 18px 20px;
}
.sl-bp-n {
  display: block;
  font-size: 12px;
  letter-spacing: 0.14em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  opacity: 0.7;
  margin-bottom: 6px;
}
.sl-bp-list li strong {
  display: block;
  font-size: 18px;
  margin-bottom: 6px;
}
.sl-bp-list li p {
  margin: 0;
  line-height: 1.6;
}
.sl-bp-foot {
  border-top: 1px solid var(--rd-line, hsl(var(--border)));
  padding-top: 24px;
  display: grid;
  gap: 16px;
}
@media (max-width: 640px) {
  .sl-bp-head {
    flex-direction: column;
  }
}

import { useEffect, useRef } from "react";
import type { SectionPhase } from "./section";

/**
 * One quiet element that moves with her voice, and the only evidence on screen
 * that the microphone is alive.
 *
 * For twenty-one minutes she talks into a blank card. The recorder already
 * reports a REFUSED microphone, but it cannot see a live-but-silent one -- a
 * muted input, a device the OS switched underneath her, a headset that dropped
 * off. That failure produces a perfect-looking run, a clean upload, and a
 * Report Card of zeros, and she finds out at the end of the twenty-one minutes.
 *
 * So this does two jobs with one reading.
 *
 * **Presence.** A bar that answers "is anything reaching it" without answering
 * "how am I doing". Deliberately not a level meter with a target: nothing here
 * may look like a score, because nothing is judged until the section ends.
 *
 * **The fault.** If the input never leaves the noise floor across a whole
 * stimulus, that is said out loud at the boundary, while there is still a
 * section left to save -- and unsaid the moment anything is heard again.
 *
 * Nothing here re-renders the runner. The bar is written to as a DOM node
 * inside an animation frame, because a component that set state at 60Hz would
 * be re-rendering the drill room underneath her while she reads.
 */

/**
 * The line between "an open microphone in a quiet room" and "nothing arriving".
 *
 * RMS over the raw time-domain samples, so it is amplitude and not loudness. A
 * genuinely dead input sits at or near zero; a live one in a silent room still
 * carries the room. Set low on purpose: this exists to catch nothing at all,
 * never to judge a quiet speaker, and a false "your microphone is dead" during
 * a real run is worse than the fault it reports.
 */
const NOISE_FLOOR = 0.004;

export function SectionStage({
  stimulus,
  phase,
  stimulusNumber,
  totalStimuli,
  error,
  onExit,
  elapsedSeconds,

  const noKeyboard = useNoKeyboard();
  // Position across the section, drawn as the rail's fill. Progress inside the
  // current stimulus is deliberately not shown: the app does not know it.
  const through = Math.min(stimulusNumber - 1, totalStimuli) / Math.max(totalStimuli, 1);

  return (
    <div className="ac-section">
      {/* The white rail, as on the real section: chrome above the paper, never
          on it. It carries where she is and the way out, and nothing else. */}
      <header className="ac-section-head">
        <span className="ac-section-where">
          Stimulus <b>{stimulusNumber}</b> of {totalStimuli}
        </span>
        <div className="ac-section-rail" aria-hidden>
          <span className="ac-section-rail-fill" style={{ transform: `scaleX(${through})` }} />
        </div>
        {/*
          * Elapsed, counting UP, against the goal.
          *
          * This screen carried no clock at all, on the reasoning that a running
          * clock produces the rushed reading the drill exists to treat. The
          * drill is against the clock, though, and a student with no way to see
          * the time cannot pace a 25-minute section. Counting up rather than
          * down is the compromise: she can see where she is without a number
          * falling towards zero in the corner of her eye.
          */}
        {elapsedSeconds !== undefined && (
          <span className="ac-section-clock" aria-live="off">
            {clock(elapsedSeconds)}
          </span>
        )}
        {onExit ? (
          <button type="button" className="ac-section-exit" onClick={onExit}>
            Leave
          </button>
        ) : (
          <span />
        )}
      </header>

cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"

if [ "${SKIP_TYPECHECK:-0}" != "1" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] Typechecking (npm run typecheck)..."

BASE=$(git merge-base HEAD origin/main)

if [ "$LOCAL" = "$REMOTE" ]; then

elif [ "$REMOTE" = "$BASE" ]; then

else

URL: https://lsatprep.study/activities/3982be97-6957-4223-8350-c607218199da
Source element: <body>
---
READ, RECALL, COLOUR
Read, Recall, Colour · PT21 Section 3

Read it, tell it back, react to it. Then the stimulus disappears and you say the whole argument back from memory.

Start the section
Back

Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start.

WHAT YOU DO, STEP BY STEP
STEP 1
Read it.

Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.

STEP 2
Tell it back.

Look away from the screen and tell it back in your own words, out loud, like you are telling a friend what it said. Out of order is fine. Casual is fine. Laughing is fine.

STEP 3
Colour it.

React. A doubt, a question, an example, a joke, a feeling. One or two seconds is plenty. It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours.

STEP 4
The Recall.

Press space. The stimulus disappears and does not come back. Now say the entire argument back from memory, out loud, with every detail you can still hold. This is the part that gets scored.

Read the long version

When you are ready, press Start. The clock begins counting up and the recording begins.

Start the section

## user

Tool result:
.task: Work through the stimulus out loud, a piece at a time. When you have got all of it, press space. The text goes away, and you say the entire argument back from memory.
.coach.drill: a whole Logical Reasoning section run out loud, where the student reads each line of a stimulus, looks away, tells it back from memory in her own words, reacts to it, and gives The Recall of the whole stimulus before moving to the next one
.coach.rubric[0]: Judge by meaning, never by wording. Her own words are required here, so a good recall will never match the line's phrasing. High overlap with the original is the failure, not the standard.
.coach.rubric[1]: Order is free. She is encouraged to deliver the ideas out of the order she read them in, and reordering must never cost her anything.
.coach.rubric[2]: Casual, unpolished, laughing delivery is expected. Never mark down for style, length or fluency.
.coach.rubric[3]: A detail merely gestured at is not carried. Naming the topic is not the same as saying what was claimed about it.
.coach.rubric[4]: Numbers, names, dates, quantities and connecting words are each their own detail. Never treat one as carried because the sentence around it was.
.coach.rubric[5]: A reaction that could be pasted onto any other sentence does not count as a colour. Judge whether it engages this content, never whether it is correct or well put.
.coach.rubric[6]: Score The Recall against the captured key for that stimulus, not against a retelling. A different name for the same move is fine; missing the move is not.
.coach.rubric[7]: Silence during a silent read is correct and expected. Only silence inside a recall or a colour counts against her.
.rules[0]: Everything here is out loud. The whole section records in one take, and it never pauses.
.rules[1]: Read silently, about a sentence at a time. When you are not sure how much to take on, take less.
.rules[2]: Look away and tell it back in your own words. Then react to it. Both out loud.
.rules[3]: Keep going like that through the whole stimulus.
.rules[4]: Press space when you have got all of it. The stimulus vanishes and stays gone. Now say the whole argument back from memory, with every detail you can hold.
.rules[5]: Press space again and the next one loads. No breaks, no going back, all the way to the end.
.tagline: Read it, tell it back, react to it. Then the stimulus disappears and you say the whole argument back from memory.
.briefing.lede: Worth reading properly once. After that, skim the rules above and go.
.briefing.closer: One a day holds you steady. The second one is where you improve.
.briefing.sections[0].body[0]: You follow a stimulus, sentence by sentence, and it all makes sense as it goes. Then you get down to answer choice C and realise you are holding almost none of it. That is not a comprehension problem. It is a retention problem, and it is the one this drill was built for.
.briefing.sections[0].body[1]: Understanding is necessary for everything and sufficient for nothing. If the argument is not still with you while you work the answers, your understanding never got the chance to help.
.briefing.sections[0].body[2]: Most of what you call reading is built for getting to the end. Scanning names. Dropping the connecting words. Turning a list of three into one label. That is a fine way to read a phone bill. It is a bad way to read something that is about to ask you for every detail back.
.briefing.sections[0].heading: You can understand every sentence and still arrive with nothing.
.briefing.sections[1].body[0]: Three steps through the stimulus, then a fourth step once you have read all of it. You decide how much to take on at a time, and nothing on the screen is tracking where you are.
.briefing.sections[1].body[1]: Space is the only key, and you press it twice per stimulus. The first press takes the text away and asks you for the whole argument from memory. The second press moves you to the next stimulus. Nothing advances on its own, so a pause to think costs you nothing but the clock.
.briefing.sections[1].steps[0].body: Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.
.briefing.sections[1].steps[0].label: Read it.
.briefing.sections[1].steps[1].body: Look away from the screen and tell it back in your own words, out loud, like you are telling a friend what it said. Out of order is fine. Casual is fine. Laughing is fine.
.briefing.sections[1].steps[1].label: Tell it back.
.briefing.sections[1].steps[2].body: React. A doubt, a question, an example, a joke, a feeling. One or two seconds is plenty. It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours.
.briefing.sections[1].steps[2].label: Colour it.
.briefing.sections[1].steps[3].body: Press space. The stimulus disappears and does not come back. Now say the entire argument back from memory, out loud, with every detail you can still hold. This is the part that gets scored.
.briefing.sections[1].steps[3].label: The Recall.
.briefing.sections[1].heading: What you actually do.
.briefing.sections[2].body[0]: Do not give back the gist, or the summary, or the important parts. You cannot know which parts were important until you have read the whole thing, so filtering on the way through mostly means quietly throwing out the piece you were going to need.
.briefing.sections[2].body[1]: Fewer words is fine when the words cover the same ideas. Fewer ideas is not. The names, the numbers, and the small words carrying the load, all, most, only, probably, they either come back or they do not.
.briefing.sections[2].body[2]: **Efficiency is never a licence to leave information out.**
.briefing.sections[2].heading: Simpler is not the same as shorter.
.briefing.sections[3].body[0]: You will open your mouth to colour something and find nothing there. That lasts about a week.
.briefing.sections[3].body[1]: It is not that you have no thoughts. It is that nobody has ever asked you for one in the middle of reading before, and the habit takes a few sessions to wake up. Say something obvious on purpose if you have to. "Okay, sure." "That seems like a stretch." "Who measured that?" All of those count.
.briefing.sections[3].body[2]: The reaction is what makes the sentence yours instead of the author's. Skip it and you are back to repeating words you do not own.
.briefing.sections[3].heading: You will think you have nothing to say. Everybody does.
.briefing.sections[4].body[0]: While you are working the stimulus, nothing on the screen hides. The whole thing stays readable, which means the only thing stopping you rereading a line while you tell it back is you, looking away from it.
.briefing.sections[4].body[1]: That is deliberate. Reading while you recall is a one way street with cars coming both ways. It jams. You stop using your memory at all and slide straight back into the author's exact words instead of finding your own.
.briefing.sections[4].body[2]: It is also the version that still works on paper, and on test day. A screen that hides the text for you is a crutch that cannot come with you.
.briefing.sections[4].heading: Look away. That part is on you.
.briefing.sections[5].body[0]: The goal is twenty-five minutes for a whole section, and it only counts if every stimulus got recalled, coloured, and given The Recall. Finishing early with four of them skipped is not finishing early. If you have extra time on test day, your goal scales with it.
.briefing.sections[5].body[1]: The clock in the corner counts up, not down. It is there so you can pace a section, and it is deliberately not counting towards zero, because a number falling away in the corner of your eye produces exactly the skating read this drill exists to fix.
.briefing.sections[5].body[2]: It is not a race. It is the pace at which this stops being a study exercise and starts being how you read under a clock. Go slower while it is new. The speed shows up on its own once the method does.
.briefing.sections[5].heading: Twenty-five minutes, and what that number means.
